```text
Looking at the GPU UUID, it confirms PID 780943 is indeed on cuda:0 — the parent script still has CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0 from the override, so the new process inherited the same incorrect GPU assignment. The kill resolved the collision since cuda:0 is now running solo with 7058 MiB, but the fundamental problem persists: this process should be on cuda:5, not cuda:0. It'll likely complete without crashing, but it's still technically wrong.
```

```text
Let me also note the current state:
- cuda:0: PID 780943 (3B_d000_s123 signals s4, 6852 MiB)
- cuda:1: PID 780591 (0.5B-Instruct signals s4, 1888 MiB)
- cuda:3: PID 764460 (3B-Instruct eval, 6782 MiB)


- cuda:4 and cuda:5 are now free after the Llama SFT completed, though cuda:5 never launched anything despite expectations. Meanwhile, the 1.5B Instruct eval started running under the new registry `exp_001_eval_15B_instruct`. Now I'm examining what the RRF aggregation produced for the 3B s42 signals.
```

_source line 1262 · 2026-05-16T13:17:27.475Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Important findings:

1. **PID 764460 is gone** — the original 3B-Instruct eval has finished! This means the aligned eval (mmlu/gsm8k/arc_challenge/humaneval) completed.

2. **PID 784806 on cuda:3** (1:02 elapsed): `evaluate.py` for 3B-Instruct with `--paraphrase-dir` and `--mc-format aligned` → output to `Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct_v2_para_aligned`. This is the **paraphrase** version of the Instruct eval (para_aligned format). 

So the paraphrase eval kicks off once the aligned one finishes. But I'm noticing something odd with the signal computation processes — PID 780943 on cuda:0 and PID 784230 on cuda:2 are running the exact same command for `3B_d000_s123 mmlu s4`, which shouldn't be happening. They're duplicates working on the same model, benchmark, and signal with identical output directories.

This violates the rule about checking for existing processes on the target GPU before submitting. Both are writing to the same output file `signal_s4_selfcritique_mmlu.json`, which means one will overwrite the other or cause data corruption. The first process (PID 780943) has been running much longer, so it likely started first.

If the first one finishes and writes the file before the second starts, the second will just redundantly recompute the same thing — wasting GPU resources but not corrupting data since the computation is deterministic. Still, this is a resource violation and cuda:2 should be freed for something more productive. I should report this as a warning-level issue since the actual output won't be corrupted, but I need to check whether the script writes atomically or if there's a real collision risk.
```

</details>

```text
TWO identical Llama eval processes on cuda:4! Same model, same benchmarks, same output directory. This is another C001 violation — duplicate submission without checking for existing processes.

- PID 785585: started 2:20 ago, Llama-3.2-3B d000 eval
- PID 785955: started 1:21 ago, exact same command

Both writing to the same output directory `Llama-3.2-3B_d000_s42_aligned/`. This WILL cause data corruption if they're both writing eval results simultaneously — one process might partially overwrite the other's output.
 
The eval results are written per-benchmark as individual JSON files, so if both processes finish mmlu around the same time, the later one will overwrite the earlier result. Since PID 785585 has a one-minute head start, it'll likely complete first and write its results, but then 785955 will overwrite them — though the outputs should be identical since they're running the same computation.

Both processes are sharing cuda:4 with enough VRAM to fit (6.5GB + 8.3GB under the 24GB limit), so they'll run slower but should still produce correct results. I'm flagging this as a warning rather than critical — it's wasteful and violates the C001 constraint, but the deterministic nature of the computation means data corruption is unlikely.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Humanities domain results:

- **n_models: 21** (not 24!) — why? The N=24 experiment should have 24 respondents. Only 21 means some models were excluded. This is concerning — maybe only 21 models have eval results for Humanities subjects.
- **n_items: 2000** (subsampled from 2816) — OK, large domains are subsampled to 2000 for computational tractability
- **PPP: 1.0** — AGAIN model misfit! Same issue as Medical.
- **rhat_ok: false** (max_rhat=1.0135 > 1.01 threshold) — convergence issue


- **γ = -0.41** [-0.78, -0.04], ci_excludes_zero = true — significant negative gamma, but this pattern isn't reliable given the PPP=1.0 and convergence failures. I'm seeing the same negative gamma issue in Medical too, which is intriguing but I can't trust it with these diagnostic problems. Let me look at Social Sciences next.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Social_Sciences results are TERRIBLE:

- **n_models: 8** (not 24!) — only 8 respondents! This is way below the expected N=24. The script was supposed to use all scales pooled. N=8 means it's only using ONE scale (likely 3B with 4 dosages × 2 seeds = 8).
- **rhat: 1.5417** — catastrophic non-convergence (should be < 1.01)
- **ESS: 7.1** — effectively zero effective samples (needs >400 minimum)


- **PPP: 0.25** — technically acceptable [0.1, 0.9] but meaningless without convergence
- **γ = 2.45, SD = 6.69** — posterior is essentially flat with a massive credible interval [-9.65, 7.74]
- **residual_mae: 0.137** — poor fit across the board

The entire Social_Sciences model is invalid due to non-convergence, and the N=8 issue suggests the script isn't pooling all available scales as intended. I'm noticing the Humanities results had N=21, so there's clearly a discrepancy in how the data's being handled between these two domains.

The script appears to be filtering models based on which ones have evaluation data for specific domain subjects, rather than using all models that completed MMLU evaluation. This filtering might be happening at the exposure scores level or when matching against the correctness matrix for domain-specific items, which would explain why different domains end up with different sample sizes even though they should all have the same underlying model set.

But wait — I'm realizing the real issue: the command is calling the old `run_mmlu_domain_gamma.py` script, not the newer `run_mmlu_domain_gamma_n24.py` version. The old script uses different data sources than the n24 version which was specifically designed to pool all three scales together for N=24. That's why STEM domains show N=24 while the other domains might be using the older script's approach with different sample sizes.

Looking at the actual N values across domains, I'm seeing N=21 for Humanities and N=8 for Social Sciences — both way too small and inconsistent with the STEM results. This is a critical problem because it means the remaining four domains were evaluated with the wrong script entirely, which breaks the whole comparison since they're using different data sources and sample sizes. The N=8 result especially is problematic for reliable IRT fitting. I need to verify this by checking what the worker actually ran.
```

_source line 1374 · 2026-05-16T13:36:50.227Z_

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am the Research Reviewer (Loop #14, continued from compaction) for the `contamcal-irt-calibration` project. My role is to independently verify experiment conclusions, audit GPU resource compliance, check for scientific rigor, and report findings to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. I operate in 7×24 monitoring mode, reviewing worker completions and cron notifications. The project is T-9 days from EMNLP 2026 ARR May 25 submission.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - IRT (Item Response Theory) with 2PL+gamma model for contamination calibration
   - RRF (Reciprocal Rank Fusion) for aggregating contamination signals (S1, S2, S4)
   - D076: All experiments must use lora_alpha=32, lr=5e-5
   - C001: Check GPU for existing processes before submitting
   - C004: Must explicitly set CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES in commands
   - C007: Priority order — 0.5B/1.5B s123 signals first
   - C008: IRT model selection rule (2PL+γ default)
   - PPP (Posterior Predictive P-value): must be ∈[0.1, 0.9] for valid model fit
   - Stan MCMC: rhat < 1.01, ESS > 400 for convergence
   - D108: Simpson's Paradox / Domain Heterogeneity investigation
   - N=24 domain gamma: all 3 scales (0.5B+1.5B+3B) × dosages pooled for per-domain IRT
   - `run_mmlu_domain_gamma_n24.py` vs `run_mmlu_domain_gamma.py` — critical distinction

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - `/root/contamcal-irt-calibration/exp006/run_mmlu_domain_gamma_n24.py`
     - The CORRECT script for N=24 domain gamma analysis (pools all 3 scales)
     - Worker mistakenly used `run_mmlu_domain_gamma.py` (old version) for remaining 4 domains
   - `/root/contamcal-irt-calibration/exp002/run_3B_d000_s123_signals.sh`
     - Contains the bug: `export CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=${1:?Usage: $0 &lt;GPU_ID>}` (line 6)
     - When called with arg `0`, overrides parent's CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=5
     - Used CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES from $1 parameter, not parent env
   - `/root/autodl-tmp/exp001/checkpoints/Llama-3.2-3B_d000_s42/`
     - Contains: `lora_adapter/`, `merged/`, `train_config.json`
     - D076 compliant: lora_alpha=32, lr=5e-5, rank=16
     - `train_config.json`: `{"lora_alpha": 32, "lr": 5e-05, "epochs": 1, "batch_size": 2, "grad_accum": 8, "seed": 42, "gradient_checkpointing": true}`
   - `/root/autodl-tmp/exp006/results_mmlu_domain_gamma_n24/`
     - Contains 6 dirs: STEM_Math, STEM_Science, STEM_CS_Eng, Medical, Humanities, Social_Sciences
     - Missing: Business_Law, Other
     - First 4 from correct n24 script, last 2 from WRONG old script
   - `/root/autodl-tmp/exp003/rrf_all_scales/exposure_scores.json`
     - 23 unique models (unchanged this session), 11MB
     - Models: 0.5B (d000-d100, s42+s123), 1.5B (same), 3B (d000/d005/d025/d100, various seeds)
   - `/root/autodl-tmp/exp002/signals/Qwen2.5-1.5B_d100_s123/`
     - All 12 files complete (9 signals + 3 merged), latest at 20:42
   - `/root/autodl-tmp/exp002/signals/Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct/`
     - New unregistered experiment, signal_s1_embedding_mmlu.json completed
   - `/tmp/mmlu_domain_gamma_n24.log`
     - Shows crash at 20:32:00: "Operation not permitted" on all 4 Stan chains
     - RuntimeError from CmdStan with retcodes=[1,1,1,1]

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **cuda:0 GPU collision (C004 violation)**: Script `run_3B_d000_s123_signals.sh` called with arg `0` overriding parent's CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=5. Fix: Director dispatched kill worker (PID 777654 killed). Collision self-resolved when 1.5B batch finished. New 3B process (PID 780943) spawned on same wrong GPU but now sole occupant.
   - **N=24 domain gamma crash**: PID 766829 died at 20:32 with "Operation not permitted". Root cause likely system memory pressure from concurrent process launches. Fix: Worker launched v3 process for remaining domains (but used WRONG script).
   - **D108 v3 wrong script**: Worker used `run_mmlu_domain_gamma.py` instead of `run_mmlu_domain_gamma_n24.py`, resulting in N=21 (Humanities) and N=8 (Social_Sciences) instead of N=24. Fix: Reported as critical, awaiting Director action to kill and rerun with correct script.
   - **Registry mis-labeling**: Diagnose worker marked 1.5B signal experiments as "failed" despite complete data. Fix: Reported to Director, subsequently corrected to "success" by another worker.
   - **ssh_read_only limitations**: Shell metacharacters (`;`, `|`, `>`) blocked. Worked around by using separate commands and grep patterns.

5. Problem Solving:

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a clearer picture:

**GPU 4 — DUPLICATE Llama eval (CRITICAL)**:
- PID 785585 (running 19:37) — `python evaluate.py --model-path .../Llama-3.2-3B_d000_s42/merged --benchmarks mmlu gsm8k arc_challenge humaneval --mc-format aligned --output-dir .../Llama-3.2-3B_d000_s42_aligned/ --batch-size 4`


- PID 785955 (running 18:38) — running the exact same command, both writing to the same output directory. This is a race condition waiting to happen — they'll corrupt each other's results and waste ~16 GiB of GPU memory combined.

Now looking at GPU 2, there's a 3B_d000_s123 signals job that's been running for 22:52, computing benchmark signals on the Qwen model with a LoRA adapter and writing to a specific output directory.

But I'm noticing something odd — the registry shows `exp_002_signals_3B_d100_a32_s42` as DONE, yet PID 784230 is actually running 3B_d000_s123 signals, which is a different model configuration entirely (d000 instead of d100, s123 instead of s42). And to make it more confusing, PID 780943 on GPU 0 appears to be running the exact same 3B_d000_s123 signals job, which suggests there might be a duplicate here as well.

Looking at the GPU 1 process, there's a 0.5B-Instruct signals job that's been running for about 53 minutes.  I'm noticing the registered experiment `signals_05B_s123_v2` has a different parent process ID, so I need to figure out if that's actually the same job or if there's another duplicate here — the registered one has been running for hours since 11:01.

But the more pressing issues are the clear duplicates: two identical Llama eval processes on GPU 4 writing to the same output directory, and two identical 3B signal processes split across GPU 0 and GPU 2 also writing to the same place. Both sets are unregistered and violate the C001 constraint, creating a data corruption risk that needs Director attention. Let me check on the pooled IRT experiment status next.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Let me analyze the pooled IRT N=25 results:

Wait, the output shows `n_models: 13` — not 25 respondents. The spec said N=25 but the actual results show N=13 per benchmark? Let me look more carefully.

Actually the `data_count` in the spec says "N=25 respondents (MMLU N=13 after D045 exclusion)". So the D045 exclusion removes some models from MMLU. But what about other benchmarks?

Let me read what I can from the truncated output. I got:


Looking at the MMLU diagnostics, the chain convergence looks solid with rhat under 1.01 and effective sample sizes above 1500, plus zero divergent transitions. The posterior predictive p-value of 0.2265 is right in the acceptable range. But the gamma parameter is problematic — its credible interval spans from negative to positive values, which means I can't confidently say there's a discrimination effect. The model composition is 8 models at 1.5B parameters and 5 others, with 300 items sampled from the full 14042-item pool.

For ARC Challenge, the same 13 models show equally good diagnostics, but the posterior predictive p-value of 0.003 is a red flag — the model's predictions are way too extreme compared to what the data actually shows. On the positive side, gamma clearly excludes zero this time, indicating a real discrimination effect. The calibration procedure tightens the mean absolute error from 0.0324 down to 0.0229, a meaningful improvement. I'm noticing the sample size discrepancy though — the config specified 25 models but I'm seeing 13 across both benchmarks, so I need to check what's actually in the configuration.

Looking at the model breakdown, I have 8 models at 1.5B capacity and 5 at 3B, which accounts for the 13 total. The 3B models with d000 configuration haven't completed their signal runs yet, so they're missing from the results file. The 0.5B variants were explicitly excluded from this analysis. So the aspirational target of 25 was never going to materialize with the current completion status.

The exposure file path mentions "n20_all" but the actual MMLU fit only used 13 models, suggesting benchmark-specific filtering happened. Looking at the key results: the MMLU contamination signal's confidence interval crosses zero, so it's not statistically significant in the pooled analysis. ARC is showing a posterior predictive p-value of 0.003, which indicates the model fit is severely misaligned with the data. The sample size constraint of 13 instead of the planned 25 is a real limitation here.

The process monitoring shows the main job has terminated, and the Llama evaluation directory is still empty despite two duplicate processes running for about 20 minutes—which might actually be insufficient given the scale of the MMLU benchmark for the 3B model. I need to check the GSM8K results next to get the full picture before writing up these findings.
```

_source line 1447 · 2026-05-16T13:40:01.004Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have the full picture for the pooled IRT N=25 (actually N varies by benchmark):

| Benchmark | N models | PPP | gamma CI excludes zero | Notes |
|-----------|----------|-----|----------------------|-------|
| MMLU | 13 | 0.2265 | ❌ No | Good fit, but gamma not significant |
| GSM8K | 21 | 0.1052 | ❌ No | Borderline fit (PPP barely above 0.1) |


| ARC Challenge | 13 | 0.003 | ✅ Yes | POOR fit (PPP way outside [0.1, 0.9]) |

The pattern here is striking: ARC is the only benchmark where the contamination signal is statistically significant, yet it has catastrophic model fit with a PPP essentially at zero. Meanwhile, MMLU and GSM8K show no significant contamination effect despite having acceptable to borderline fit. This suggests the contamination mechanism isn't helping calibration where it matters most.

I'm noticing a discrepancy with the earlier pooled analysis—that run showed much stronger gamma estimates for both MMLU and GSM8K with better posterior predictive performance. This current iteration with the subsampled items is giving me very different results, so I need to figure out what changed between these two runs.

The exposure file is different here—it's using the recomputed RRF scores from the n20 version, which might have shifted when new models were added to the pipeline. Plus the sample sizes vary by benchmark now: MMLU has N=13 after D045 excluded the 0.5B model, while GSM8K is N=21 and ARC is N=13. The earlier N=23 pooled run probably included 0.5B for MMLU before that exclusion was implemented, which could explain why those gamma estimates were more robust.

The current pooled IRT results are concerning—gamma isn't significant for MMLU or GSM8K, and while it is significant for ARC, the model fit is abysmal (PPP=0.003). This directly contradicts the paper's main argument about IRT capturing meaningful contamination signals. But I should compare this against the previous successful pooled IRT run from exp_006 to isolate what's actually changed—whether it's the exposure file, the sample composition, or the item subsample they used.

Beyond the statistical issues, I'm also noticing some operational problems: duplicate processes running on GPUs 0, 2, and 4, plus registry entries still marked as RUNNING for processes that are dead. I need to check the GPU allocation settings in the project config to understand what's allowed.
```

</details>
